- 1.130 (2026-11-08): **The repair loop starts itself when there is work, an input error is not "severe", and a provider timeout is retried before it is dismissed.** Three admin-panel defects the owner hit. **(1) Severity.** `classify_severity` matched "500" inside an amount (`credits 26500`), so a trial balance that does not balance was logged **SEVERE** and drove a ten-problem generation run. It now treats an input error ("does not balance", "not an accounting problem", "names no transaction") and a transient provider failure (timeout, rate limit, unreachable) as **light**, and "500" only as an HTTP status, never inside a figure. **(2) Idle loop.** The repair loop only ran when someone pressed Start, so a queue of open errors sat beside an "idle" status. `bp.server.serve` now starts it at boot when there are replayable errors (opt out with `BP_AUTORESEARCH_AUTOSTART=0`), and the admin page starts it once per load when it sees open errors and a stopped loop. **(3) Timeout.** `diagnose` checked "could not read this as a journal" before "timeout", so a provider `TimeoutError` was classified as a parse failure and left open. Timeout and unreachable now win over the parse cue; `resolve` retries the stored problem once on the fix model (silently, so a second timeout adds no new error) and only then dismisses it. **(4) Queue drain.** With the loop finally running, its 3600 s cycle meant ~40 open errors would take 40 hours. The cycle is now **30 s**, `_pick_highest_impact_error` takes an `exclude` set so an error already attempted this session is skipped rather than retried forever, and an error the loop tried and could not fix is marked **attended** so it waits for the owner instead of blocking the queue. The loop's "errors closed %" read **0** however much it repaired, because the metric counted only the open list: a closed error left both the numerator and the denominator. It now counts every logged error. **(5) Panel noise.** The server logged every 4xx with an error payload as a pipeline failure, so an unknown deliverable, a malformed case id or a ticker the provider cannot price filled the panel with errors no replay could repair. It now logs a 4xx only on a pipeline path (`/chat/`, `/statements`, `/frameworks/`); a 5xx is still logged everywhere. `tests/conftest.py` also gives the suite its own `BP_OPS_LOG`, so a test that exercises a refusal on purpose no longer writes into the owner's live log. Tests: `tests/test_product_case_loop.py` gains severity, provider-timeout and queue-skip assertions, 25 there in all; `tests/test_product_instrument_refusal.py` asserts a refused price writes no error record; the fixer + docs sweep passes (36).

- 1.129 (2026-11-08): **A generated trial balance that does not balance is an invalid fixture, not a pipeline failure — and the self-heal recognises a dropped account.** The self-heal harness showed 1/10 passing because the **problem generator** wrote trial balances whose two sides did not agree (e.g. debits 26,420 vs credits 26,500), and each one was logged as a pipeline error and handed to the fixer. Now: the generator's prompt requires every trial balance it writes to balance, `chat_heal._valid_problems` drops any generated TB that does not, and `solve_problems` classifies a "does not balance" refusal as `invalid` (no pipeline error is logged) instead of `error`. The admin page excludes invalid fixtures from the pass rate, so the score reflects the pipeline, not the generator's arithmetic. Separately, the fixer now *resolves* an unbalanced trial balance when the cause is a dropped account: `_try_fix_unbalanced` reads the problem, finds the imbalance, matches a skipped line whose amount equals it, types the label from its keywords (`_guess_rule`), adds the rule to the tier-1 vocabulary, and re-reads — accepting the fix only if the two sides now agree, and removing a wrong guess (`tier1_tables.remove_rule`). A balanced problem that carries adjustments is checked by the *adjusted* route, so its unadjusted TB is not mistaken for an imbalance. `diagnose` recognises `unbalanced_input` and `timeout`; a genuinely unbalanced problem with no recognisable skipped line is marked **invalid** and closed, and a timeout is dismissed as transient. `_statement_models` bounds the extract provider at 40 s per attempt × 2 retries (under the admin page's 120 s abort), with a 4096-token output budget. Proved: `Cash 10,000 / Capital 8,000 Cr / Deferred grant 2,000 Cr` is recognised as a liability, and the problem then solves (assets 10,000 = liabilities 2,000 + equity 8,000); the 26,420/26,500 generated problem is classified `invalid` and leaves the queue. Tests: `tests/test_product_case_loop.py` gains `FixerClassificationTest` (5) and `InvalidFixtureTest` (4), 23 there in all; the statements + fixer sweep passes (149 + 8 subtests).

- 1.128 (2026-11-08): **The local server serves the site Cloudflare publishes.** Cloudflare Pages publishes the whole `web/` directory, but the local workbench served only `/`, `/account.html`, `/admin-sbw.html`, `/pricing.html` and `/assets/*`. So the page's own fallback - `/subjects.json`, the baked subject snapshots the published site reads when no API answers - was a 404 at `127.0.0.1:8000`, and any other file the site carries was unreachable locally: the two addresses were not the same site. `bp/server.py` now serves any file that exists under `web/`, after every `/api/` route, so a file can never shadow an API route. The name is resolved and checked to stay inside `web/`, so a traversal attempt reads nothing else on the machine. One tree, one set of URLs: `/subjects.json`, `/subject-*.json`, `/preview.html` and the rest answer at both addresses. Four tests in `tests/test_session_api.py` pin it: the snapshot is byte-equal to the file, a baked subject page is served, an encoded traversal is refused, and an unknown `/api/` route still answers JSON. Suite: 4341 tests, 0 failures, 2 skipped.
- 1.128 (2026-11-08): **A third-party problem bundle is accepted as tile cases.** `case_upload.upload` now recognises a `{topic, problems: [...]}` file and stores one case per problem, mapping the third party's field names onto the method's own (breakeven: `selling_price` -> `price`, `fixed_costs` -> `fixed`, `actual_units` -> `expected_units`, `contribution_per_unit` -> `contribution_margin`, `break_even_units` -> `breakeven_units`, `margin_of_safety_ratio_pct` -> `margin_of_safety_ratio` as a decimal). A problem the engine cannot represent (two periods, several products) is skipped with a written reason rather than failing the file. A refusal case (`reject: true`) is stored with `status: "error"` when the engine refuses its inputs. The third party's own answers are kept as the expected result where they agree with the engine; where a percentage is given to two decimals it is compared in its own units and absorbed only when it is a rounding, never when it is a real mistake. The file the user supplied imports as 8 passing cases (two problems are inherently multi-period or multi-product and are skipped with a reason). The bundle's own `topic` chooses the tile — `break-even` lands in `breakeven`, `financial statement ratios` in `ratios` — and the form's tile selection is only the fallback when the bundle names no known topic. Tests: `tests/test_product_case_upload.py` (8).

- 1.127 (2026-11-08): **A stored case replays on every computation tile, and the case authoring brief is written.** The tile-case runner refused any tile without published defaults *before* it looked at the case's own inputs, so every accounting case — 18 of them — replayed as `needs_input` even when its figures were complete and its expected result was the core's own output. `paper_batch.generate_one` now takes the case's inputs when they are supplied and asks for defaults only for a preview with no inputs; all 18 accounting cases replay green. Two tests pin it: a depreciation case with inputs passes on a tile that has no preset, and a preview with no inputs still returns `needs_input`. **`docs/CASE_AUTHORING_BRIEF.md`** is the handover for an authoring model: the case-file contract (`bp-tile-case/1`, every expected number copied from the frozen core, never hand-computed), the ten-case design per tile (baseline, exam prose, boundary, large values, adverse, rates, two-period, repeated structure, refusal, free choice), the tile/method/input table for the accounting shelf, the statements-service exception with its HTTP route and `core.scalars`, and the acceptance commands. A refusal case is `{"status": "error"}` with no result — the only refusal shape the tile-case path produces. Tests: `tests/test_product_case_loop.py` (14); the accounting cases pass 18/18.
- 1.126 (2026-11-08): **The test suite is offline again, and the slices follow the library.** A suite run on a machine that serves the product was reaching the network from inside its tests. `bp.server` maps `Backend/.env` onto `BP_LLM_PROVIDER`, `BP_OPENGO_API_KEY` and their siblings at import, so the analysis lane drafted paragraphs no test expected and the proposal lane waited on HTTPS: one acceptance module took 873 s and two runs of the same template no longer produced the same bytes. `tests/__init__.py` and `conftest.py` now set those names **empty** before any test module imports, and `bp.server` reads a present-but-empty name as "not configured" rather than falling back to the file; a test that wants a model patches `prose.available` and `prose._call`, as `tests/test_matrix_assist.py` does. That module now runs in 3 s and the byte-stability test holds. The calculator and explainer slices are regenerated against the current library (211 methods), and the ratio golden's `not_computed` lists carry the market, Altman, dividend and accrual ratios the catalogue now defines - no computed ratio changed. Two page labels the tests pin are restored: "Download the explanation" on the computation card and "Download financial statements (Word)" on the accounting card. Suite: 4335 tests, 0 failures, 2 skipped.

- 1.125 (2026-11-08): **Finance and economics read the problem, and every file is named for its shelf.** Two gaps closed, one shared. **(1) A reader for every shelf.** `bp/product/problem_reader.py` and `POST /api/cases/solve/read` read a method's declared figures from a pasted problem on the accounting rules: a figure is taken only when a field's own name — the schema's `desc`, the key written as words, or a registered synonym ("hurdle rate", "cost of capital", "initial outlay") — sits next to it; a `%` becomes a decimal only for a field the schema calls a rate and only when the sign is explicit; a typed figure wins and is not re-read; a required field that was not read comes back as a named gap; numbers no field claimed are counted, so the page can say they were not used; the reader computes nothing. Finance tool pages gain a **Paste the problem** card with *Read the figures*, which fills the form and lists what is still not stated. Economics reads through the same synonym table, so a problem worded with "hurdle rate" or "price index" is read instead of refused. **(2) One name for every file.** `downloadName` in the page names each saved file `subject_subtile_NNN.ext`, with the number a per-subject-and-subtile counter kept in the browser: `Accounting_Statements_001.docx`, `Accounting_Trial balance check_002.xlsx`, `Finance_Capital budgeting_001.xlsx`, `Economics_Inflation and real interest_001.docx`, `Case_<title>_001.docx`. Every surface that saves a file goes through it — statements, workbook, paper, explainer, check, economics, case report. Honest scope: finance still asks the student to choose the tool (no automatic problem-type classifier), and this is the accounting *reading* standard applied to the two shelves, not a re-implementation of every accounting engine. Measured live: an NPV problem read to `rate 0.1` and the four cash flows, ran, and saved as `Finance_Capital budgeting_001.xlsx`; an inflation problem saved as `Economics_Inflation and real interest_001.docx`; the statements tile as `Accounting_Statements_001.docx`. Tests: `tests/test_problem_reader.py` (11), `tests/test_export_downloads.py` E-dl-010; `qa/check_page_syntax.py` 4 of 4.

- 1.124 (2026-11-08): **The page loses its explanatory padding, Method folds away, and the trial-balance check downloads too.** The owner asked for a clean page: no sentence under a heading that only repeats the heading. **(1) The shelf and tiles.** `subjectCard` and the subject header no longer render a shelf summary ("Ledger, statements, ratios and the cost-volume-profit questions built on them." is gone), `subjectTiles` no longer prints `tile.note` ("The tile does the bookkeeping and shows its working; it never asks you to balance anything."), and the "Catalogued frameworks with no worksheet yet…" and reading-lens paragraphs are removed — the cards' own badges say what they are. The catalogue keeps every summary and note for the API; the page renders none. **(2) The tool page.** The objective subtitle ("Compute the material, labour and overhead variances for the period"), the whole "Before you start" card (the worked-example sentence and its paragraph) and the management-accounts "These are the figures the engine will post" line are deleted; the Clear-the-example button moves into the header row beside Back and Calculator. **(3) Method** is a collapsed `<details>` beside *Draft work plan*, so the form is not buried under three reference cards. **(4) The trial-balance check** gains the download row — spreadsheet, the check (Word), and the explainer — and sets `state.session` so the Word route has a live session. Verified on the served page: no shelf summary, no tile note, no worked-example line, no Method card (the `<details>` is there instead), and the check's three downloads answered 200 (`…-workpapers.xlsx`, `Computation trial balance check.docx`, the explainer). Tests: the three stale presence guards are updated to the owner's rule (`test_product_subjects` twice, `test_subject_accounting_acceptance`); 149 pass in those files, and `qa/check_page_syntax.py` parses 4 of 4.

- 1.123 (2026-11-08): **The accounting downloads actually download.** The management-accounts panel's statements button posted to `/api/cases//export` with the *empty* case id and a server-side `destination`, so it answered 422 and nothing reached the browser — the same defect class as E-dl-001, which had been fixed everywhere but there. `exportStatements` now saves the case and asks for the bytes (`{kind: "docx", deliverable: "statements", download: true}`), and a new **`statements` deliverable** (`docx`, `html`; blocks `["statements"]`) renders the statements alone — no workings, checks or report — so the button named "Download the statements" produces exactly that. The panel's four buttons are now guarded one by one, and the dead `#tostats` button left in the results markup is removed. **The tool page carries its own result.** A delivered run used to go silent on the page it was run from — the report and the files appeared only on the Result page — so `renderNew` now renders a result card with the report and the download row (plus the statements button for management accounts). **The downloads are readable at a glance.** A `.btn.dl` style paints every download control solid black with white 700-weight text, and it is applied wherever a result downloads: the result card, the Business Calculator, the tool page, the management-accounts panel, the case report, the statements tile and the chat/plan/economics paper buttons. Measured against a live engagement: `statements.docx`, `workpapers.xlsx`, the session paper and the explainer each returned 200 with a filename, and the three buttons compute to `rgb(0, 0, 0)` on `rgb(255, 255, 255)` at weight 700. Tests: `tests/test_export_downloads.py` (E-dl-007 the route, E-dl-008 the page, E-dl-009 the tool page), `tests/test_exports_credits.py` (the statements profile); 191 pass with 190 subtests.

- 1.122 (2026-11-08): **The result card reads top-down, the downloads moved under the numbers, and the tier badge is gone.** Six UI corrections the owner reported while working the accounting tiles, applied through the shared builder so no surface can drift. **(1) The badge.** The upper-right tier badge ("Custom analysis" / "Verified calculation") is removed from the delivered result (`resultBodyHtml`) and the case report (`renderCase`); the technical record keeps its `Tier` row, and the phrase "custom analysis" is gone from the management-accounts guide and the T1/T2 legend. **(2) The downloads.** Download spreadsheet, Download the paper (Word) and Download the explainer now sit at the bottom of the computation card, below *Key numbers* and the regression block, instead of in the top action row with Save as case and the Gantt controls. **(3) The explainer** is present again on both the Result page and the Business Calculator and posts to `/api/cases/{id}/explanation`. **(4) The three notes.** Honesty, Limitations and Assumptions render as collapsed `<details class="studynote">` panels; each honesty item's first letter is capitalised, and the text is unchanged. **(5) Draft work plan** is a collapsed `<details>` in `renderNew`, so every tile that freezes a spec folds it away. **(6) Management accounts** gains the full download row — Download financial statements, Download spreadsheet, Download the paper (Word), Download the explainer — on the delivered panel. **(7) The case report** moves its own download row (Excel, Word, HTML, explainer, worksheet) from the top of the Report tab to the bottom, after the text, the accounting blocks, the folded notes and the technical record, so every surface ends with its files. Verified in the running workbench: the result card has no badge, Key numbers precedes the downloads, all three `<details>` open and close, and the management-accounts panel exposes all four buttons with handlers bound. `qa/check_page_syntax.py`: 4 of 4 blocks; `tests/test_product_subjects.py`, `tests/test_product_statements_tile.py`, `tests/test_product_statements_api.py`: 81 pass.

- 1.121 (2026-11-08): **The open gaps close: full text vocabulary, a unified problem-type classifier, an opening-balances policy key, and live OpenCode Go credentials.** Four gaps the last build named are closed. **(1) Vocabulary.** Every raw input the 128 computation specs declare — 168 distinct names that are never another spec's output — now has a parse pattern in `COMMON_PATTERNS` (`_EXTRA_PATTERNS` + a `_p()` helper building a word-bounded `label [of|is|was|:] amount` pattern). The "131 input names with no parse pattern" gap is gone; two false-positive traps are fixed (`depreciation_expense` no longer reads "accumulated depreciation", `payments` no longer reads "repayments"). **(2) Classifier.** `src/bp/product/problem_type.py` names the Tip 5 problem type (1–7) in every record as `tip5_type`: error correction, missing raw figure, ratio analysis, reconstruction, trial balance, cash-flow statement, integrated case, preparing from transactions. A text no cue matches is `type=None`, never a guess. **(3) Policy layer.** The extract contract gains a `balances` key for opening balances, `to_opening()` converts it to one opening entry (`opening: true`) so the trial balance carries it but the cash-flow movement does not, and `_solve_narrative` passes it through. **(4) Live reader.** `bp.server` loads OpenCode Go credentials from `Backend/.env` when the `BP_*` names are unset (`OPENCODE_API_KEY` → `BP_OPENGO_API_KEY`, `LLM_PROVIDER` → `BP_LLM_PROVIDER`, `PROSE_MODEL` → `BP_LLM_MODEL`), so live mode works without a manual export. The case solver also learned to name a stated figure no entry uses (`unused_amounts`) and, when a model is configured, to hand the whole narrative to the reader rather than answer with half a ledger. Proved live: a grant-and-van narrative ("received a government grant of 50,000 and bought a delivery van for 30,000") returned other income 50,000, total assets 50,000, 11/11 checks. Tests: `tests/test_financial_engines.py` (37, including vocabulary and classifier contracts); the statements sweep passes (285 + 8 subtests); the frozen fixture regenerates byte-identically.
- 1.120 (2026-11-08): **A pareto, a work breakdown, a native chart and a figure sheet.** Four gaps close in the visual layer, none of them by letting a model draw or add anything up. **Pareto** (`charts.py`) is a chart kind drawn from **two stored series**: each entry names its bar artifact and its running-total artifact, and the drawing only checks that the running total never falls. The line is scaled to its own stored maximum, so the renderer never sums the bars to make the curve it then displays. Plotly draws the same two series on a secondary axis; the workbook draws them natively. **A work breakdown** (`diagrams.py`, kind `wbs`) is a nested `root`/`children` tree with optional codes: a code may carry digits (`1.1.2`), a label may not, five levels is the limit, and the leaves are laid out across the page with their parents centred over them. **Native Excel charts** (`spreadsheets.py`): a chart figure is now written as cells plus a `c:chartSpace` part - the labels and values sit on the sheet above a `xdr:graphicFrame`, the chart caches the record's own strings and references those cells, so Excel redraws it and a reader can check every figure in the cells. Bar, line, stacked bar, pie and pareto are native (pareto as a bar-plus-line combo on two axes); a waterfall, a gantt and a table keep their picture, because there is no faithful native shape. **A figure sheet** (`visuals.export_sheet`, `POST /api/cases/visuals/sheet`): several stored visuals on one Word page, in the order given, each drawn from its own record. The chart chat also takes a third column for a pareto and offers the kind. 36 new tests.


- 1.119 (2026-11-08): **The four financial-statement problem engines: reconstruction, error correction, integrated case, and the 22 guards.** Four capabilities close the "handle any statement problem without fabricating" gap — three deterministic engines and one guard runner. **Reconstruction** (`src/bp/product/reconstruction.py`) solves a missing figure from a stated relation: linear identities (COGS = BI + purchases − EI, A = L + E, ending RE) solve for any variable, ratio identities (current ratio, D/E, ROA, ROE) solve for the ratio, numerator or denominator, and `solve_all` chains solved values across relations so COGS feeds gross profit feeds gross margin. **Error correction** (`src/bp/product/error_correction.py`) detects wrong-entry patterns (equipment expensed, unearned revenue, depreciation omitted, ending inventory overstated, accrued expense omitted, capex charged to repairs, prepaid expensed in full, sale/purchase/interest/wages not recorded) and builds the correcting entry plus its signed effect on net income, assets, liabilities and equity; a stated original-vs-correct pair is also supported. **Integrated case solver** (`src/bp/case_solver.py`) runs the nine-stage state machine INGEST → PARSE → DETECT → RESOLVE → JOURNALIZE → POST → ADJUST → PROJECT → VALIDATE, records every stage, and uses the deterministic template layer so it needs no model. Two detector gaps were found and closed while proving it: `sold goods for X cash` was matched as an asset disposal (crediting PPE) and is now `goods_sale_cash`; a bare `started the business with X cash` had no detector and is now `capital_contribution`; `paid rent of X` now matches `rent_payment2`. **Guards G1–G22** (`src/bp/guards.py`) run every Tip 7 guard over the frozen record and report pass/fail/not-applicable; the correctness guards block the record, the trust guards are recorded for the audit trail. All four are wired into the tile (`statements.py`: `_solve_reconstruction`, `_solve_error_correction`, `_solve_case`, `_apply_guards`) and injected from `bp.server` so the product package imports no core module. Tests: `tests/test_financial_engines.py` (27); the statements sweep passes (251 + 8 subtests).

- 1.116 (2026-11-07): **The Economics shelf starts with three service tiles.** Macroeconomics, Microeconomics and Managerial economics are now full service tiles that follow the financial-statements pattern: choose a problem type, declare the figures, optionally paste the instruction, solve, and download the Word file. `economics.py` classifies the selected type, reads the figures from the instruction by the field's own label (manual fields stay collapsed for correction), gates an instruction that names a different problem, runs the frozen method, and renders the problem, method, declared figures, result, economic interpretation, assumptions and limitations. Microeconomics absorbs the old Market basics and Firm behaviour groups; Managerial economics absorbs pricing, demand, cost-benefit, cost-effectiveness, SROI, unit economics and break-even from Quantitative core; both old tiles are removed. The individual calculators remain inside each service tile. Tests: `tests/test_economics_service.py` (11).

- 1.115 (2026-11-07): **Uploaded tile cases and Excel previews.** The Tile cases card accepts a file per subject: `.json` (case schema), `.csv`/`.tsv`, `.xlsx`/`.xlsm` (first worksheet), `.docx`, `.pdf`, `.txt`/`.md` and images. Tables map their headers onto the method's input names automatically. Every file is gated before it is stored: the inputs must pass `methods.validate_inputs` and the method must run, so a case that cannot replay is rejected with the method's own message and never written. A stored case replays its own instruction and inputs (not the tile preset). The run panel now selects stored cases, and optionally generates 1–10 fresh problems on the subject shelf and solves them in the same run. The paper preview gained a Format selector (Word / Excel / Both): each tile can write a real `.xlsx` workbook, and the case runner verifies its Summary, Inputs, Results and Record sheets. Tests: `tests/test_product_case_upload.py` (5).

- 1.111 (2026-11-07): **Tile cases and a token-free self-healing loop.** `bp/product/case_store.py` seeds one case per testable tile under `qa/cases/<subject>/<tile>/case_NNN.json`: the tile's prefilled instruction, its declared figures, and the frozen method's own result. `bp/product/case_runner.py` replays a case through the paper path and checks the computation (numeric tolerance for Decimal trailing digits) and the paper's required phrases. A failing case logs one `tile-case` error carrying its `case_id` and the instruction. `auto_resolve.resolve` now replays a case by code and marks it verified only when it passes — zero model tokens. `autoresearch` skips an error with neither a problem nor a case id without a model call, uses the flash → pro ladder only for a stored problem, and uses errors-closed % as its metric instead of running four pytest suites. When no unattended error remains it replays the whole stored case suite (246 cases, ~1 minute) and stops only when that passes; one attempt per error per cycle. The admin page gains a **Tile cases** card (seed, run a subject or all, results). Tests: `tests/test_product_case_loop.py` (5).

- 1.121 (2026-11-08): **Beneish M-score from two statement records.** `statements.beneish(records)` derives the eight indices (DSRI, GMI, AQI, SGI, DEPI, SGAI, LVGI, TATA) from two solved records, names a missing figure, and calls the frozen `beneish_m_score`; `POST /api/cases/statements/beneish` serves it. `altman_z_score` and `beneish_m_score` now use Decimal constants, so the `_d` repair's Decimal inputs do not break the formulas. `RATIO_GAPS` points Beneish at the two-record route. Tests: `tests/test_beneish.py` (4); the suite passes and both fixtures are in sync.

- 1.120 (2026-11-08): **Piotroski is computed from two statement records, and the finance shelf's `_d` helper accepts both call shapes.** `statements.piotroski(records, extra)` builds the nine Piotroski inputs from two solved records (prior and current) plus a declared diluted share count, orders them by period, refuses a pair from the same period, and calls the frozen `piotroski_f_score`; `POST /api/cases/statements/piotroski` serves it. `finance_methods._d` now accepts `_d(inputs, "key")` and `_d(value)`, repairing 78 two-argument calls (Altman, Piotroski, Beneish and the rest) that failed at runtime against a one-argument helper. Tests: `tests/test_piotroski.py` (3); the suite passes and the frozen contract is in sync.

- 1.119 (2026-11-08): **Three gaps closed: the market/Altman ratios, integrated transaction+adjustment narratives, and tax reconciliation (B-052 resumed).** The ratio engine adds earnings per share, book value per share, price-earnings, market-to-book, dividend payout and yield from declared share data, and the Altman Z-score from the statement figures plus a declared market value; `ratios(record, extra)` accepts the declared share data. The detector layer now reads "bought", "earned … cash sales" and "paid … rent", de-duplicates overlapping matches, and the transactions route applies the adjustment parser to one narrative, so a transactions+adjustments case solves without a model. `tax_reconciliation` (T1) reconciles a declared accounting profit to taxable income through non-deductible, non-taxable and allowance items, then computes current tax, deferred tax, the total charge, the effective rate and VAT payable; `statements.tax_note(record, extra)` and `POST /api/cases/statements/{id}/tax` serve it. Tests: `test_ratio_catalogue` (4), `test_statements_reader_fixes` (7), `test_statements_figures` (22); the accounting sweep passes (576 + 186 subtests); both fixtures regenerate.

- 1.118 (2026-11-08): **The adjustment reader reads a full TB-plus-adjustments narrative without the word "adjustments", and the ratio catalogue gains the cash-flow and accruals ratios.** `_split_tb_and_adjustments` separates the balance rows from the adjustment sentences (an adjustment sentence never parses as a balance row); `_adjustment_specs` now recognises prepaid amortisation (payment × months expired / total), straight-line depreciation from a cost and life, bad debts at a rate of receivables, interest accrual from principal/rate/months, and income tax at a rate, in addition to supplies, unearned revenue and accrued expenses. A problem with only adjustments names the missing unadjusted trial balance. `_RATIOS` now defines operating cash flow ratio, cash flow margin, cash flow to debt, free cash flow margin and the accruals ratio; `figures()` supplies `operating_cash_flow` and `capital_expenditure` from a present cash flow and withholds them when the cash flow is withheld. The static `RATIO_GAPS` no longer names a ratio that is computed. Tests: `tests/test_statements_reader_fixes.py` (6) and `tests/test_ratio_catalogue.py` (3); the accounting sweep passes (572 + 186 subtests); both fixtures regenerate.

- 1.117 (2026-11-07): **The deterministic reader accepts bare lists and exam preambles, refuses foreign currencies, and no longer crashes on a ratio problem.** `parse_rows` splits a line on sentence boundaries (so "…31 December 2026. Cash 10,000. Capital 10,000." reads as two accounts) without splitting decimals or dot leaders; `read_deterministic` treats a bare list of accounts and balances as a trial balance when at least two place; `BLOCKED` now catches common foreign currency codes and symbols and refuses with the USD message; a `tier1_gaps` NameError in the financial-computations route is fixed. The adjustments narrative still needs the model reader. Tests: `tests/test_statements_reader_fixes.py` (4); the accounting sweep passes (489 + 186 subtests); the frozen fixture is in sync; the page's four inline blocks parse.

- 1.116 (2026-11-07): **Test isolation for the statements core.** `statements.reset_core()` detaches every injected callable; the statement tests that attach a builder or a trend method reset it in `tearDownClass`, and the API route suite resets it in `attach()`. Without this, a test that attached `build` left it available to a later test that assumes only the four minimum callables, so a shape meant to refuse solved instead. No product behaviour changed. Tests: the accounting sweep (485 + 186 subtests) and the targeted suites (337 + 8).

- 1.115 (2026-11-07): **Two accounting correctness bugs fixed: contra-income closing and stated totals.** A contra-income account (sales returns) was closed on the wrong side, so it stayed open after closing and retained earnings was over-credited; the closing entry now posts on the side opposite each account's normal side and balances into retained earnings whichever side the net falls on. A stated balance-sheet total ("current liabilities 25,000") with no components was ignored and the analysis computed zero; a stated total is now used when its components are absent, and components still win when both are present. The statements-record fixture is regenerated by its own script. Tests: `tests/test_accounting_correctness_traps.py` (3); the eighteen suites pass (370 + 8 subtests); the frozen fixture is in sync; the page's four inline blocks parse.

- 1.114 (2026-11-07): **The broken finance cycle function is fixed, and trend consumes several statements.** `finance_methods.cash_conversion_cycle` called `_d(inputs, "dso")` against a one-argument `_d`; it now reads `inputs["dso"]`/`["dio"]`/`["dpo"]`. `statements.trend(records)` builds the per-period series (revenue, cost of sales, operating expenses derived from the expense breakdown, net income, optional total assets and equity) from two or more solved records, orders them by the year each statement ends, refuses duplicate years, and calls the frozen `trend_analysis`. `POST /api/cases/statements/trend` with `{records: [id, id]}` serves it; the statements panel gains a **Trend** button. The `derived` map now also carries `operating_expenses` with its basis. Tests: `tests/test_statements_figures.py` (20); the thirteen suites pass (351 + 8 subtests); the frozen fixture is in sync; the page's four inline blocks parse.

- 1.113 (2026-11-07): **A cycle-only frozen method, so a statement can produce the cash conversion cycle without order-quantity terms.** `working_capital_cycle` (T1) computes DSO, DIO, DPO, the cash conversion cycle and net working capital from revenue, cost of sales, inventory, receivables and payables — the figures a statement always carries. The combined `working_capital` method still needs the six operating terms. `statements.cycle(record)` and `GET /api/cases/statements/{id}/cycle` serve it, and the statements panel gains a **Cash conversion cycle** button. The method is registered as `working_capital_cycle` because `finance_methods` already owns a different `cash_conversion_cycle` id. The frozen contract is regenerated by its own script (`scripts/econ_shelf.py --fixture`, 210 methods, in sync); the subject guard declares the method a statement-registered helper, like `eps`. Tests: `tests/test_statements_figures.py` (17); the eleven statement/subject/contract suites pass (270 + 8 subtests); the page's four inline blocks parse.

- 1.112 (2026-11-07): **DuPont and working capital consume a statement; the bundle carries the income split.** `statements.figures()` now adds a `derived` map (`pretax_income`, `ebit`, each with the identity that produced it) and reads `tax_expense`/`interest_expense` from the record's own expense groups. `statements.dupont(record)` runs the three-step form from revenue, net income, total assets and equity, and the five-step form only when EBIT/pre-tax/tax are present (otherwise named not-run). `statements.working_capital(record, extra)` feeds the cycle figures from the statement and names the six operating terms it cannot supply (annual demand, order cost, holding cost, discount, credit terms, discount days). Routes: `GET /api/cases/statements/{id}/dupont`, `POST /api/cases/statements/{id}/working-capital`; the statements panel gains **DuPont** and **Working capital** buttons. Tests: `tests/test_statements_figures.py` (15); the ten statement/subject suites pass (265 + 8 subtests); the page's four inline blocks parse.

- 1.111 (2026-11-07): **The calculators report to the library, and the ratios tile consumes a statement.** Every confirmed accounting calculator run (`scv`, `abc`, `salesvar`, `workingcapital`, `proforma`, `tax`, break-even) is recorded under its shelf via `statement_library.record_calculator`, so `gap_report(group=...)` and `by_group` cover every accounting tile, not just the statements tile; a method that is not an accounting tool is not recorded. `statements.ratios(record)` computes the full ratio pack on demand from `figures(record)` and the route `GET /api/cases/statements/{id}/ratios` serves it; the statements panel gains a **Ratios** button. `ratio_pack` and `ratios` share `_ratio_rows`, so the two paths cannot format differently. Tests: `tests/test_statements_figures.py` (9); the ten statement/subject suites pass (259 + 8 subtests); the page's four inline blocks parse.

- 1.110 (2026-11-07): **The statement record becomes a shared input, and the library learns by accounting shelf.** `statements.figures(record)` turns a solved record into a stable bundle — the named figures (revenue, net income, current assets/liabilities, inventory, receivables, payables, total assets/liabilities, equity) plus the structured income, balance-sheet, equity and cash-flow groups and the named ratio gaps — so the ratio, DuPont, trend, working-capital, pro-forma and tax tiles consume the same figures the paper would print, with no re-typing and no recomputation. New route `GET /api/cases/statements/{id}/figures`. `statement_library.record(..., group=...)` and `record_problem(group, ...)` tag a problem with its accounting shelf; `gap_report()` gains `by_group` and a `group=` filter. The statements tile records as `Statements`; the calculators call `record_problem` with their group. Also guards a NULL `missing` field in `most_repeated`. Tests: `tests/test_statements_figures.py` (5); the eight statement suites pass (191 + 8 subtests).

- 1.109 (2026-11-07): **The statement library stops inventing a gap, and a ratio gate tells the truth about itself.** `statements.ADJUSTMENT_CUES` + `_needs_adjustments()` decide whether a problem actually mentions adjustments (accrued, prepaid, depreciation, allowance, warranty, shrinkage, ...). `_solve_transactions` no longer writes "no adjusting entries were derived" (it already returned `None` on an adjustment cue, so the note was always a false positive there); `_solve_narrative` writes it only when a cue is present. The ratio gate is renamed `current_ratio_equals_current_assets_divided_by_current_liabilities`, matching the division its check performs. Trap test: `tests/test_statements_gap_noise.py` (2); the six statement suites pass (186 + 8 subtests).

- 1.112 (2026-11-07): **A visual no longer needs a paper: the visual store, three sources, a proposed chart and five exports.** A student who wants one chart can make it, keep it and download it without starting a case or a draft.

  - **`visuals.py` owns the store.** A visual lives in its own `state/visuals/` store, not in a case: `id`, `title`, `source`, `kind`, `created_at`, the figure record and the bundle it was drawn from. `save`, `load`, `listing` and `delete` are atomic and deterministic.
  - **Three sources, each labelled.** *Declared* - figures the student types, labelled "Figures you declared" and never merged into a case; *calculation* - one frozen method run on declared inputs, labelled "Computed by <method> on your inputs"; *case* - artifacts already recorded in a case, labelled "Recorded in this case".
  - **The chart is proposed by rule, then confirmed.** `propose()` ranks at most three kinds from the data's shape and the student's own words: a fall suggests a waterfall, a mix a pie, a trend a line, and a bar is always offered. A model never chooses a chart and never writes a number.
  - **Five exports from one record:** PNG, SVG, a Word figure sheet, a one-slide PowerPoint, and a PDF printed by Chrome.
  - **Routes:** `POST /api/cases/visuals/plan`, `POST /api/cases/visuals`, `GET /api/cases/visuals`, `GET /api/cases/visuals/{id}`, `POST /api/cases/visuals/{id}/export`, `DELETE /api/cases/visuals/{id}`.
  - **The Home page carries the visual card.** Type `label, value` per line, preview the chart, create it, and download PNG, SVG, Word, PowerPoint or PDF. The preview is drawn but not stored; only Create writes a record.
  - **The label rule now accepts a recorded label verbatim.** A label may carry a digit when it equals the stored value of the artifact it names (`"Q1"` against a recorded `"Q1"`), as well as when the value is one of the label's numbers (`"Sprint 3"` against `"3"`).

- 1.108 (2026-11-07): **A complete project plan: charter → WBS → CPM → cost → RACI → risk.** `project_plan.py` owns the record and the deterministic half: WBS validation, work-package cost roll-up, the frozen CPM adapter, RACI and risk checks. `chat.PROFILE_PROJECT_PLAN` composes thirteen sections in one Word document with tables for the charter, scope, WBS, schedule, cost, resources, RACI matrix and risk register. The plan card carries a project-record editor (JSON or simple CSV upload). No cost, duration, owner or date is invented; a missing one is a named gate problem. Tests: `tests/test_project_plan.py` (14), the profile-door contract extended to the fifth document.

- 1.107 (2026-11-07): **Automated testing is the writer's own papers, one per subject tile.** The admin Self-Healing page's old pytest button row is replaced by a per-subject section (11 subjects, permanent) whose tile groups are collapsible (50 subsections) and whose tiles are checkboxes (246 with published defaults). A tick survives switching subsection and a page reload. **Generate selected** drives `bp/product/paper_batch.py`: each tile's prefilled instruction is its published preset objective and declared figures, the frozen method is run once, the paper is drafted by the conductor with no framework wrapper, and the corpus supplies a citable sentence per matched page; no corpus match is a named gap. Results stream into the section, and the download at the bottom of the section is a ZIP of the `.docx` papers, a combined DOCX, `instructions.md` (the prefilled instruction for every tile) and an index. Routes: `GET /api/ops/papers/tiles`, `POST /api/ops/papers/batch`, `GET /api/ops/papers/batch/{id}`, `GET /api/ops/papers/batch/{id}/download`, `GET /api/ops/papers/runs`. A full sweep of the 246 tiles with published defaults generated 246 papers with no failure. The chart-label fix (a result key such as `count_1` no longer produces the forbidden label "Count 1") is included. Tests: `tests/test_product_paper_batch.py` (5).

- 1.106 (2026-11-07): **Length is a body word count, not a band.** The student states the number the rubric asks for (600–50,000); the old short/mid/long names remain only for saved cases. `length.py` owns the unit: body words exclude the title page, the title, the headings and the reference list (the writer emits no footnotes part). A `word_target` report carries the two-sided ±10% range and a note; the allowance is strived for, not guaranteed, and length is **not** an export gate. One bounded repair pass adds whole analytical sections when short, or removes only the sections it added when long; it measures once more and stops. The completion full paper scales its per-section budgets to the target, and the worksheet, plan card and Home composer now take a number. Tests: `tests/test_word_count_length.py` (11), `tests/test_plan_length.py` numeric case, all completion and rubric suites green.

- 1.105 (2026-11-07): **The tree is green: 4067 tests, no failures, no errors.** The correctness pass first, then the remaining failures.

  - **The product boundary holds again.** `completion.py` no longer imports `bp.llm`; the provider factory is injected through `api.attach_models`, wired in `server.py` from `llm.from_env`.
  - **A saved figure reaches the paper and the workbook.** `docx.render_bytes(..., figures=...)` embeds pictures in the plain report; `figures.prepare()` re-draws a stored record from current values; `deliverables.write_export` passes them for `docx` and `xlsx`. A case with no figures keeps its exact old bytes.
  - **The drifted artefacts are regenerated:** `calc_inputs_v0_1.json` and `explain_contracts_v0_1.json` (209 methods), `web/subjects.json` and the per-subject pages, `frozen_contract.json`.
  - **The 67 finance methods declare a contract.** `finance_methods._reg` derives each input schema from the keys the function reads, and declares `unsupported_use`, `defaults`, `assumptions`, `limitations` and a `typing` precision hook, so the method audits pass.
  - **Stale expectations updated to the shipped shelves:** the accounting page (18 tools, 7 groups), the finance page groups, the calculator inventory, the gap wording ("What to state"), and the suppressed tile headings.
  - **Two order-dependent tests made hermetic:** the statements API clears model environment variables, and the Brave?Firecrawl test clears the module's 24-hour cooldown and the search cache.

- 1.104 (2026-11-07): **A figure is one record, drawn only from stored values, and it reaches the paper, the deck and the workbook.** The visual layer: charts, diagrams, a slide deck, charts in a spreadsheet, auto-triggers, a figure API and its page control.

  - **`figures.py` owns the record.** `id`, `kind`, `spec` (`bp-chart/1` or `bp-diagram/1`), `caption`, `note`, `sources`, `why`, `place`, and a `digest` of the stored values it was drawn from. A caption or a note may not contain a digit; a figure without a `why` is refused. `stale()` compares the digest with the record, so a figure left behind by an edited input is detectable. `Registry` is the one place a case's figures live.
  - **`figure_plan.py` proposes only what the data earns.** A declared series of two points is a bar, three or more a line; a composition is a stacked bar; alternatives are a bar; a CPM record becomes a Gantt from its stored early dates. Every proposal carries the reason it fired, every block that drew nothing is named with the reason, and a figure whose section the paper does not carry is reported as unplaced rather than dropped into an arbitrary heading.
  - **`renderers.py` is the engine dispatch.** The builtin standard-library SVG always runs; Plotly draws charts to PNG through `kaleido`; the headless Chrome kaleido carries rasterizes the builtin SVG (diagrams, Gantt, table); `cairosvg` is tried first and skipped when its native cairo DLL is absent. A missing engine costs polish, never the document.
  - **`theme.py` is one visual language.** The palette is defined once; Plotly reads it as a template, Graphviz as graph/node/edge attributes, Mermaid as `themeVariables`, and the builtin SVG reads `SERIES` directly.
  - **`deck.py` writes a real `.pptx`.** A slide is a claim, not a paragraph: `title`, `claim`, `figure`, `section`; every content slide must carry a claim; a figure is named by id and resolved from the registry. Speaker notes ship as `notesSlide` parts with their own master and relationships; a slide fades by default and `transition="none"` opts out.
  - **`docx.py` and `spreadsheets.py` carry the picture.** A rendered PNG becomes an inline `<w:drawing>` in the paper and an `xdr:oneCellAnchor` drawing in the workbook; with no rasterizer the paper prints the stored values as a table under the caption. The worksheet's `<drawing>` child sits where `CT_Worksheet` requires it, and a workbook with no figure keeps the bytes it had.
  - **The presentation is offered and priced.** `deliverables.PROFILES["presentation"]` now offers `pptx` as its default and `write_export` builds it; the export route carries the `pptx` suffix and MIME; credits were already priced at 3.
  - **A figure can be created in the chat.** `POST /api/cases/{id}/figures` builds and draws a figure, returns the SVG preview, and stores the record on the case only when `confirm` is true; `GET` lists them. The case keeps the record, never the bytes. The Deliverables panel now shows the Charts card with a caption field and a **Save to this case** button.
  - **Four test errors are fixed.** `capabilities.set_enabled` no longer refuses a *drop* because the capability does not fit the paper (excluding something is the student's decision, recorded); the chat test helper answers whatever the conductor asks instead of a hardcoded order; and the session test skips a method registered by an optional module rather than failing the run.

- 1.103 (2026-11-07): **The Finance shelf opens on its tiles: the Rates and FX panel folds, the Student guide and the shelf summary go, and the page's script parses again.** Three changes to the Finance subject page, and one defect that made every button on it dead.

  - **The Rates and FX panel is a disclosure.** It sat open above the tiles on every visit. `ratesPanel()` now returns `<details class="marketpanel">` with a `<summary>` header and no `open` attribute, so the shelf opens on its tiles and the reader unfolds the lookups when they need a figure. The intro sentence, both `.lookupcard`s, every id (`fxbase`, `fxquote`, `fxdate`, `rfmat`, `fxlookup`, `rflookup`, `ratesout`) and the read-then-type instruction are unchanged, so no market-data test loosens. The CSS is child-scoped (`.marketpanel > summary`), so the economics macro panel and the portfolio-book securities panel keep their plain boxes. The `/api/currencies` fill is deferred to the first open, and the lookup handlers still bind eagerly.
  - **The Student guide is gone from the shelf page.** Its block - the guidance sentence, the *Start here* path and *What this shelf does not do* - is removed from `renderSubject()`. The same guidance is still in `docs/FINANCE_STUDENT_GUIDE.md` and in the baked subject data, so nothing is lost; it is simply no longer printed above the tiles.
  - **The Finance shelf summary is gone.** *"Corporate finance, valuation and the investments, debt, risk and international tools a finance student needs."* no longer appears under the shelf heading or on the all-subjects card. The page suppresses it at render time through a new `shelfSummary()` helper (one shelf id, `SHELVES_WITHOUT_SUMMARY`), and the baked `__SBW_SUBJECTS__` block carries an empty string. **The catalogue itself still holds the sentence** - `src/bp/product/subjects.py` and the two baked snapshots are unchanged, because that is the computation owner's lane; the handover for it is in `docs/WORKLOG.md`.
  - **The "published method" instruction is gone.** *"These run a published method. Supply the figures and confirm; the server computes the result and its checks."* no longer appears under the *Quantitative Finance* heading. It was presentational text in the inline script only, not catalogue data, so no baked snapshot or `src/bp/product/subjects.py` entry needed changing.
  - **A syntax error that killed every button on the page.** Removing the Student guide block left a dangling `) +` in `renderSubject()` - no opening parenthesis matched it. The whole inline script failed to parse, so no handler was ever registered and the finance button and every other control on the page were dead. The remnant is gone; `tests/test_web_inline_script.py::test_the_inline_script_parses` now guards it, and `qa/manual/finance_page_probe.py` drives a real browser to prove the buttons work.
  - **Tests.** `tests/test_market_panels.py` gains `test_rates_and_fx_is_a_disclosure_that_starts_closed` (12 tests in that file). `tests/test_web_inline_script.py` and `tests/test_market_panels.py` pass together: 14 tests, OK.
  - **The Economics shelf is quieter.** The top-of-shelf lookup panel drops "(retrieved, not verified)" from its heading and the World Bank introductory paragraph. The tiles section drops its section header ("The tiles"), its explanatory paragraph, every "Before you start:" line, every "Calculators: supply the figures..." line, and every tile topic description. The tile headings themselves remain as clickable cards; only the surrounding instructional text is gone. Nothing in `src/bp/product/subjects.py`, the baked snapshots, or any frozen-core entry changed.

  - **Not moved.** No method, preset, input, check, computed number or frozen-core entry changed; the macro and securities panels are visually identical to before. The two failures in `tests/test_web_static_snapshot.py` are pre-existing and unrelated: both compare `web/subjects.json` against `bp.product.subjects.catalogue()` and never read `web/index.html`.

- 1.102 (2026-11-06): **the statements workbook opens in Excel.** The tile's `.xlsx` download was a valid zip of valid XML that Excel refused - "we found a problem with some content", and an empty workbook. The package was written twice, by two writers, and the tile's writer was the broken one. There is now one writer, and the tile's workbook is the workbook the case bundle already shipped.

  - **What Excel was refusing, exactly.** Three defects, each enough on its own: `/xl/theme/theme1.xml`, `/docProps/core.xml` and `/docProps/app.xml` were declared **twice** in `[Content_Types].xml` (a part carries one content type, not two); the `Notes` sheet appeared **twice** (Excel refuses a duplicate sheet name); and `<cols>` was written **after** `</sheetData>` in every worksheet, while `CT_Worksheet` requires `cols` before `sheetData`. The file was also not byte-stable: `writestr` without a timestamp writes the current time into the zip. The API's response was never wrong - it was a valid package that Excel would not open.
  - **One writer, not two.** `spreadsheets.py` already wrote a workbook Excel accepts; `xlsx.py` wrote a second, broken one. The packaging now lives in one place, `spreadsheets.write_xlsx(sheets, places)`, and `xlsx.py` is the statements tile's adapter over it. The case bundle's workbook is **byte-identical** to before (sha256 `801218277774fd0f...`), so nothing a student already downloaded moved.
  - **The workbook the student receives is the one the brief asks for.** One sheet per requested statement (`Income statement`, `Changes in equity`, `Balance sheet`, `Cash flow`), then `Working`, then `Notes` - the names the render brief gives (`docs/MINIMAX_STATEMENT_RENDER.md` section 9), so the tile's download and the case bundle's workbook agree. Amounts are **numbers**, in the record's decimal places (`#,##0.00;(#,##0.00)`), so Excel totals and sorts them; the header row is frozen; column A is sized to the longest label; no cell is merged. The statement of changes in equity now carries its movement figures as numbers with the note beside them, where the old writer left the whole line as text, and the period line reads the record's period instead of `Period:  to `.
  - **A figure the record only renders is not re-typed.** A record whose appendix cannot supply a statement's typed figures (the thinner record shapes the tile also stores) gets that statement as the record rendered it, verbatim. Re-reading a rendered line is how a figure stops being the record's, so the line stays text rather than becoming a number the record does not hold as a value.
  - **The package is guarded now.** `tests/test_statements_xlsx_export.py` (24 tests) checks the rules Excel enforces - a part declared once, a relationship that resolves, a unique legal sheet name, the worksheet children in order - and the workbook itself: the sheet set, typed amounts, the record's places, the frozen header, no merged cell, byte-stability, and no figure the record does not hold. `qa/manual/audit_xlsx_structure.py` runs the same audit as a probe against an openpyxl reference, and `qa/manual/excel_open_check.ps1` opens the file in Excel itself.
  - **Nothing else moved.** No method, preset, input, check, computed number or frozen-core entry changed. The suite holds 3899 tests; the pre-existing failures recorded in `docs/OPEN_ISSUES.md` item 18 are unchanged by this build.

- 1.101 (2026-11-06): **the site is published, and it stands on its own without the backend.** Cloudflare Pages now serves `business_pipeline_v0.12/business_pipeline/web` from `smartbusinesswriter2026-bit/smart-businesswriter002` on branch `main`, with an empty build command and that directory as the output, so a push to `main` deploys the site. The public host is static: no Python backend stands behind it, so the page reads the subject list baked into the site.

  - **The subject rail reads a baked snapshot when the API cannot answer.** `web/subjects.json` holds exactly what `bp.product.subjects.catalogue()` returns, and `web/index.html` reads it when `/api/subjects` is unreachable. On the published site that path is the normal path, not the exception.
  - **The snapshot cannot drift.** `scripts/build_web_subjects_snapshot.py` regenerates it from the code, and `tests/test_web_static_snapshot.py` fails if the committed file and the code disagree, if the format changes, if the snapshot holds no subject, or if the page loses the fallback.
  - **The page no longer reports a failed local API on a host that never had one.** The "the local API did not answer completely" banner is raised only when *some* calls failed, not when every call failed, because a static host answers none of them.
  - **Nothing else moved.** No method, preset, input, check, computed number or frozen-core entry changed. The suite holds 3873 tests; the pre-existing failures recorded in `docs/OPEN_ISSUES.md` item 18 are unchanged by this build.

- 1.100 (2026-11-05): **`origin/main` is merged into this branch, and this branch stays the single line.** `main` and `corpus/merge-v1` had grown apart: 25 commits on `main` and 317 on this branch, touching the same files, with fourteen conflicts. The merge is resolved, and every side's unique work is kept.

  - **What `main` had that this branch did not, and is now here.** The **People & Organization - Strategy & Design** subject (Lewin, Galbraith, Mintzberg) with its worksheets and before-briefings, its catalogue slice and registration module, its own root `CHANGELOG.md` / `DECISIONS.md`, and the `tests/`, `wiring/` and `run-suite.mjs` scaffold that came with it. The **institutional market-data keys** (`ALPHAVANTAGE_API_KEY`, `MASSIVE_API_KEY`) are now in the credential allowlist, in `Backend/.env.example`, and in the loader's status report. The **tracked-instrument store tests** (`test_product_instruments.py`: create, record, dedupe, returns series, readiness, fail-closed edges, the provider selection and the routes) are here too, in their own class rather than dropped.

  - **What this branch had that `main` did not, and keeps.** Everything the statements lane built (builds 1.98-1.99, the detector/runtime layer and the three engines), the **Portfolio Lab** (builds 1.58-1.59: the B-178 guidance, the reference list, the example books and the screening lab), the **tile Close** redesign (builds 1.66/1.68: the Close button is a named icon control in the heading's action zone, not a red button), and the **one-point font increase** (047a404).

  - **The fourteen conflicts, and how each was read.** `web/index.html` (27 hunks), `mockup/business-calculator.html` (18), `api.py` (4), `marketdata.py` (3), `instruments.py` (2), `tests/test_product_instruments.py` (2), and one each in `AGENTS.md`, `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/CONTRIBUTORS.md`, `docs/HANDOFF.md`, `docs/HOME.md`, `docs/WORKLOG.md`, `src/bp/product/source_env.py`, `tests/test_product_subjects.py`.

  - **The code took this branch's side, because this branch is the later generation of the same product.** `origin/main`'s `index.html` is 5,145 lines against this branch's 10,334, and it is the *earlier* design: its tile Close is the older `rememberTileGroup` / `closeTileView` pair, which this branch replaced with the tile-close shim (the shim keeps the same behaviour - the group is remembered on open, the exit row is right-aligned, the group is marked and scrolled to). Its font sizes are this branch's sizes plus one point on the body, which this branch then *reduced* by one point on the owner's word of 2026-11-03 and pinned in the test. Its `marketdata.py` is this branch's file without the third provider in the chain. Its `api.py` is this branch's file without the fail-closed unknown-route refusal and without the cleanup that deletes an instrument whose first price could not be sourced. Taking this branch's side loses no behaviour that `main` had and this branch did not.

  - **Two checks were ported rather than dropped.** `main`'s tile-Close check was ported onto the shim's own pieces (the same behaviour, the shim's markers). `main`'s tracked-instrument store class was kept whole, against this branch's API, which is compatible: `create`, `record`, `load`, `returns_series`, `readiness`, `delete`, `InstrumentError` and `InstrumentCorrupt` are the same, and `record()` takes the provider it was already given.

  - **One compatibility fix, because the merge put two names on one key.** `main` loads the Massive key as `BP_MASSIVE_API_KEY`; this branch reads `BP_MASSIVE_KEY`. The allowlist keeps `main`'s name (its test pins it) and the market-data layer now accepts either, so the key in `Backend/.env` reaches the provider whichever name it is loaded under. Nothing else changed: no method, preset, input, check, computed number or frozen-core entry moved.

- 1.99 (2026-11-04): **the reader stops refusing what it can read: the six seams are closed, and the tile now reads a transaction narrative with no model at all.** This build carries everything the tree gained since 1.98: the detector/runtime layer, the income-statement and balance-sheet-analysis engines, the 128-spec computation engine, and the work that closes the reader's seams. It also carries four defects in that engine that no test could have caught, because the engine had no test module.
- 1.99 (2026-11-04): **the reader stops refusing what it can read: the six seams are closed, and the tile now reads a transaction narrative with no model at all.** This build carries everything the tree gained since 1.98: the detector/runtime layer, the income-statement and balance-sheet-analysis engines, the 128-spec computation engine, and the work that closes the reader's seams. It also carries four defects in that engine that no test could have caught, because the engine had no test module.

  - **One period model, and a date is a point.** `src/bp/statement_period.py` owns the period rules. A narrative that opens "on 1 January 2025" now gets `2025-01-01` to `2025-12-31` instead of a one-day period; a fiscal year whose start and end are both stated reads as **one** period, not two; and a transaction date on a line ("On 2 January it bought a delivery van for 30,000") no longer reads as a comparative. Seven solvers used to derive their own period, which is why they disagreed; they now use this one.

  - **Distinct expense lines.** Salaries and Rent no longer collide on one "Operating expenses" line, so the most common shape in accounting — a trial balance with both — is read instead of refused. `statement_plan.DEFAULT_CHART` now **is** the detector layer's chart (`src/bp/statement_chart.py`): the tile has one chart, and every code a detector posts to has a home in it.

  - **No prose is posted as an account.** A label is matched on its account fragment, and a label that reads as a sentence is named and left out rather than rewritten into an account it does not name. "Compute the net income for Alder Trading" beside "Sales 70,000" used to make the sales line an "Other income" row that was then dropped — net sales read as 0.00 and net income as −60,000 where the answer is 10,000. It now reads Revenue 70,000.

  - **A stated partition is proved, not asserted.** "Sales of 90,000, of which 55,000 was cash" is accepted because the validator can prove the difference: both operands are in the same clause and the arithmetic checks. A difference the text never frames as a partition is still refused.

  - **The deterministic reader no longer crashes.** Four detectors read the same figure from one of two wordings, and the templates reached for the first slot alone, so the journal entry carried no amount and the posting died on an uncaught `decimal.InvalidOperation` — the whole answer died with it, on a perfectly ordinary sentence ("paid 8,000 cash salaries"). The slot that is filled now wins; a line with no amount is raised as a named `DetectError` the tile can catch, never as a conversion error it cannot.

  - **A trial balance keeps its route.** "…and also compute the liquidity ratios" used to reach the balance-sheet-analysis engine, which reads the same figures as *balances*: it printed total assets **86,900** where the trial-balance route prints **77,900** (it does not net the accumulated depreciation), and it hid the income statement. The more specific reading now wins, and the instruction gets the three statements and 13 ratios.

  - **The computation engine is built, and four of its defects are fixed.** `src/bp/product/financial_computations.py` carries 128 specs in 11 sections. One was labelled **"Peso/Dollar Change"** — a home-currency name in a global product (B-177) — and is now "Horizontal Change (absolute)". Eight input patterns captured a word instead of the number, so those figures were silently dropped. The topological sort looked dependencies up by spec id while `depends_on` names output names, so it resolved nothing and four specs recorded a gap for a value the engine had already computed. And values did not travel between sections, so seventy-two specs reported a gap for something already known. The sections are now computed in dependency order over one shared pool, and `tests/test_financial_computations.py` (new, 16 tests) holds all of it.

  - **A route that computes nothing does not answer — while the product module is unwired.** The engine was tried before the reader and returned a record even when it had computed nothing, so it answered with a sheet of gaps. It now hands the instruction back. **The test that proves it passes alone and fails in the suite**: `bp.server` wires the product module, and a wired module answers that instruction on the transactions route instead of refusing, so the test exercises a configuration the product never ships in. It is named in `docs/OPEN_ISSUES.md` (Item 18) and is not fixed here.

  - **The owner's case study is re-measured, and the manual is rewritten.** `qa/manual/statements-case-study.md` carries the current figures and says what changed; the two defects it recorded on 2026-11-03 (ratios at 28 decimals, brought-forward retained earnings labelled "other") are fixed and the case now says so. `docs/FINANCIAL_COMPUTATIONS_MANUAL.md` was rewritten: the previous version claimed "2 pre-existing failures only" where the suite on HEAD has **61**, claimed the narrative reader "REQUIRES a configured LLM" when the deterministic layer now reads a narrative, and claimed the integrated case solver was "NOT BUILT" when it is six seams, all closed.

  - **The measurement, which is the point.** The full suite was run three ways, and every tree it was run on is pinned: on a **byte-exact copy of the pre-increment commit** `d938095` (build 1.98), on a **byte-exact copy of the current HEAD** `f1bb0c5`, and on a **snapshot of the working tree**. A copy is proven byte-exact by comparing its git tree hash with its commit's own tree hash (`qa/manual/suite_treehash.py`); the suite is run by `qa/manual/suite_measure.ps1`, so the measurement is a script and not a claim. **The pre-increment baseline: 3784 tests, 25 failures and 40 errors. The current HEAD: 3828 tests, 27 failures and 38 errors. The working tree: 3828 tests, 27 failures and 38 errors.** Against the pre-increment baseline, **58 are pre-existing**, **5 are fixed by this increment** (the detector's `decimal.InvalidOperation` crash in two tests, the runtime's ok-path, the detector contract, and the served build number), and **5 are new**. **This increment adds 44 tests and five new failures**, and the five are named: three are the product-boundary rule — `src/bp/product/statements.py:1084` imports `bp.product.income_statement` absolutely, and the boundary tests read that as the product reaching into the frozen core — one is the calculator's keypad contract (`e4184a8` changed the fit rule and the committed test still asserts the old line) — and one is this increment's own new test, which passes alone and fails in the suite because `bp.server` wires the product module. The 40 errors are the plan layer's golden engagements (bad debt, OCI, revaluation, disposal, deferrals, depreciation methods) and all share one root cause: `EngagementError: chart[11]: duplicate account code 1100` — the helpers append `1100 Trade receivables` to a preset chart that now carries it already. The full separation is in `qa/baseline/seam-suite-comparison.txt`, and the five new failures are parked in `docs/OPEN_ISSUES.md` (Item 18).

- 1.98 (2026-11-04): **the statements workbook is a workbook now: one typed sheet per requested statement, then the working papers, then the notes.** The Excel export was a screenshot of text — one string per line in a single sheet, every amount stored as text, and the statement of changes in equity missing altogether. It is now a real spreadsheet, built by hand from the frozen record with `zipfile` and XML, and it presents only what the record holds.

  - **`_accounting_rows()` in `src/bp/product/spreadsheets.py` is replaced.** One sheet per requested statement (`Income statement`, `Changes in equity`, `Balance sheet`, `Cash flow`), then `Working`, then `Notes`. The label is in column A and the amount is a **number** in column B, right-aligned; detail rows indent one level, group headings are bold, subtotals are bold with a top rule and totals bold with a double bottom rule; a blank row separates sections and never separates a subtotal from its total; the header row is frozen, column A is as wide as its longest label, and no cell is merged. The amount format follows the record's decimal places (`#,##0.00;(#,##0.00)` — a negative in parentheses, the record's own convention). The case sheets (the artifact table, the report) keep the stored strings, because those values are not all numbers.
  - **The statement of changes in equity is a sheet.** It is built from `appendix["equity_statement"]` — per account, the opening balance, each movement with its kind and what it was, and the closing balance — so a reader can check opening + movements = closing line by line. The roll is not reconstructed from the balance sheet.
  - **The Working sheet** carries the journal (date, description, account, debit, credit), the trial balance (account, debit, credit, totals), the checks (name, PASS/FAIL, detail), the cash-flow reconciliation and the reconciliation statement when the record carries one. A cash flow the record withheld is not a sheet, and the working papers say why, in the record's words. The journal's hashes and entry markers are the system's, not the student's, and are not written (B-199).
  - **The Notes sheet** is the record's notes text, verbatim.
  - **The request decides the sheets.** The appendix carries the request's audit trail (`requested`, `hidden`, and the reason a cash flow was withheld), because the workbook is built from the appendix alone; a statement that was computed but not asked for is not a sheet, and an income-statement-only request produces exactly one statement sheet.
  - **The record now carries three typed blocks the renderer needs — a computation-lane carry, named here.** (1) `appendix["income_groups"]` holds the income statement's lines and totals **by the chart's statement line**. They already existed, but only as text: the record's "Operating expenses 67,500" was a line of the rendered statement, never a value, so a renderer could only have produced it by summing the accounts above — an invented figure. `ledger.income_groups()` now carries what `report.income_statement` already printed, and `report.income_statement` reads its totals from it, so the figure is computed once. (2) `appendix["engagement"]` holds the period, the currency and the places. (3) `appendix["cash_flow"]` holds `opening_cash` and `closing_cash`. No figure changed, no check was loosened, and the statement texts are byte-identical.
  - **The fixture is regenerated** (`python qa/manual/make_statements_fixture.py`): the pipeline changed intentionally, so the record's `plan_hash` moved. The frozen record now carries the blocks above.
  - **Verified.** `tests.test_statements_render_contract` → **13 tests, OK**; `tests.test_product_boundary` → OK; `qa/manual/demo_statements_workbook.py` reads the workbook back through `tests/xlsx_read.py` and proves the acceptance list end to end: typed cells on every statement sheet, the income statement foots to the record's net income, the balance sheet balances, the three cash-flow reconciliations hold, a withheld cash flow is absent with its reason on the working sheet, an IS-only request gives one statement sheet, two exports are byte-identical, and **no figure on any sheet is a figure the record does not hold**.

- 1.97 (2026-11-04): **the render lane gets a frozen record and a read-back harness.** The design lane builds the statements workbook next, and it must not build against a moving target. (1) **`tests/fixtures/statements-record.json`** is one solved engagement with all four statements rendered (income statement, changes in equity, balance sheet, cash flow), net income 52,575, total assets 178,100, cash flow 104,600 across operating/investing/financing, and all eleven core checks passing. It is built deterministically from the hand-derived golden engagement in `tests/test_statement_plan.py` through the real plan layer, with the model's extraction step stubbed by the declared journal — no key, no network, no clock. (2) **`tests/product_statements_fixture.py`** builds it and exposes `typed_numbers()`, the set of figures the record holds as values (text renderings excluded), which is what a renderer's cells are checked against. (3) **`qa/manual/make_statements_fixture.py`** regenerates the fixture; the contract test fails when the regenerated record differs, so an unintended pipeline change cannot be absorbed silently. (4) **`tests/xlsx_read.py`** unzips a workbook and returns its sheets as rows of cells (inline strings, shared strings, numeric cells), with `line_value()`/`statement_rows()`/`numbers_in_text()` for assertions. (5) **`tests/test_statements_render_contract.py`** (13 tests) proves the fixture is a complete full request, all checks pass, the three cash-flow reconciliations hold, retained earnings rolls forward, two exports are byte-identical, and **every figure in a workbook produced from the record is a figure the record holds** — the test that catches a renderer inventing a total. (6) **A finding, not fixed here:** the current Excel export omits the statement of changes in equity, because it copies `statements_export` (which carries the other three) and the equity text lives in `appendix.equity_statement_export`. `tests/test_statements_render_contract.py` names it and `docs/MINIMAX_STATEMENT_RENDER.md` adds the sheet to the brief. Suite count 3708.

- 1.96 (2026-11-04): **the Financial statements tile renders only the statements the request names, while always computing the full ledger (Tip 3 and Tip 4).** Compute scope and output scope are now separate: a "balance sheet only" request still computes the income statement and the equity roll, and the record says so. The income statement is the only statement that stands alone; a balance sheet drags in net income and retained earnings; a cash-flow request drags in the whole stack.

  - **`dependency_closure()` / `classify()` in `src/bp/statement_plan.py`.** A declared projection graph (`IS` -> `RE` -> `BS` -> `CF`) gives the topologically ordered set that must be computed, and `classify()` names the four request shapes: full-compute-partial-render, standalone IS, requires-full-ledger, requires-plugs.
  - **`present(result, problem_type, requested)` is now scoped.** The core still produces every statement; the record carries only the requested ones. The cash-flow rule is unchanged and now request-gated: it is shown only for a transactions problem and only when asked for, and a withheld cash flow carries its reason.
  - **The record carries the audit trail.** `requested`, `computed` and `hidden` sit beside `statements`: a BS-only record reports `computed: ["IS", "RE", "BS"]` and `hidden: ["IS", "RE"]`, so "where did retained earnings come from" has an answer. `warnings` names a withheld statement.
  - **`wants_statements()` reads the request from the instruction**, like `wants_ratios()`. Detection is conservative: a mention is not a request. A statement counts when its clause carries a limiting word ("only", "just", "skip", "don't show", "without") or a directive verb ("prepare", "show", "give me"). An explicit `statements` parameter wins, and accepts `IS/RE/BS/CF` or the record keys (`income_statement`, `balance_sheet`, `cash_flow`).
  - **`verify_output()` in `statements_verify.py`** checks the record against the request: every requested statement is present, nothing unrequested leaked in, and a withheld cash flow has its reason. A single-statement record is complete when that one statement is present. `_record()` refuses to freeze a record that fails it.
  - **Export can be one statement.** `POST /api/cases/statements/{id}/export` accepts `{"statement": "BS"}` and returns only that statement, with a named file; a statement the record did not render is a 409 that lists what it did render.
  - **The boundary violation from 1.88 is fixed.** `statements.py` no longer imports `bp.*` anywhere: the plan layer's `extract`/`build` are injected by `bp.server` alongside the rest of the core. This repairs five red boundary/scope tests that were failing at HEAD.
  - **A substantive plan refusal is final, and a missing model is not.** `NoModel` separates "nothing is configured" (try the next reader) from "this amount is not in the instruction" (refuse, never retry elsewhere), and a `StatementRefused` (two periods, two entities, a consolidation) is no longer softened into "the reading failed".

- 1.95 (2026-11-04): **the golden suite grows to twenty cases.** Six new cases: reducing-balance depreciation in a full engagement, units-of-production depreciation, bad debt expense with a dividend, two prepaids, accrued interest with a loss, and a half-year/pro-rata mix. Every case asserts all three articulation identities. Tip 1's "build ten or twenty of these with different features ... run them on every change" is now satisfied.

- 1.94 (2026-11-04): **tax benefit on a loss is now a registered adjustment.** `tax_benefit_on_loss` computes `-pretax x rate` when pretax is negative and zero otherwise, and is grounded, validated and named in the extraction prompt. The registry now holds nine functions. Two new tests.

- 1.93 (2026-11-04): **the golden suite grows to thirteen cases.** Six new cases exercise reducing-balance depreciation, units-of-production depreciation, bad debt expense, a prior period carrying forward (opening balances tie to prior closing), a no-adjustment engagement, and the half-year convention. Every case asserts all three articulation identities. Tip 1's "build golden test cases ... run them on every change" now has breadth, not just the base case.
